# Build Plan

Moved out of PLAN.md on 2026-10-28, unchanged, to keep PLAN.md short enough to read. PLAN.md is still the overview and the source of truth, and this file is part of it. "The Syncfusion section" meant `docs/plan/syncfusion.md`, the WPF app's Syncfusion notes, dropped with the WPF app on 2026-11-07 (the tag `wpf-final` still has them), and the WPF types, files and controls the entries name are at `wpf-final` too; a section named without a file (Domain Model, Application Architecture, Reactive Costing Cascade) is [PLAN.md](../../PLAN.md)'s.


Build phase by phase. Do not jump ahead to a later phase's concerns while working on an earlier one; add them to Open Items below instead.

| Phase | Scope | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Solution scaffold, DI host builder, ReactiveUI bootstrap, EF Core DbContext | Done |
| 2 | Units of Measure (incl. seeded, protected length units) + Ingredient & Packaging Categories | Done |
| 3 | Syncfusion UI shell — license bootstrap, custom Windows11Light theme applied, core control set wired into `TinapayanRMS.UI` | Done |
| 4 | Ingredients (Purchased/Made source) + Packaging Materials (CRUD, purchase-unit → standard-unit conversion) | Done |
| 5 | Ingredient & Packaging Price History + `CurrentCost` rollup | Done |
| 6 | Made Ingredients — BOM lines, `WeightLossPercent`, nested cost rollup with cycle check | Done |
| 7 | Sub-Assembly Roles + Sub-Assemblies + Sub-Assembly Lines (composition, cost rollup) | Done |
| 8 | Products — sub-assembly usages, packaging lines | Done |
| 9 | Costing Parameters — labor/overhead/margin/VAT → selling price | Done |
| 10 | Derived Products — Clone vs Live Template, per-tab linking | Done |
| 11 | End-to-end reactive price cascade, made-ingredient, sub-assembly and packaging branches (wiring Phases 5–10 together) | Done |
| 12 | Margin/price-trend reporting (SfChart; replaced by summary grids 2026-09-27) | Done |
| 13 | Validation (FluentValidation), logging (Serilog), UX polish | Done |
| 14 | Full test coverage consolidation | Done |
| 2026-09-14 | Sub-assemblies owned by products (made in the product, linked or copied by others; role-tab links replaced, packaging links kept); grams-only recipe lines; each usage's total weight, yield and weight per piece, the typed one kept; Roles moved to Settings, the Sub-Assemblies screen removed | Done |
| 2026-09-15 | Standalone sub-assemblies (no owner product): the Sub-Assemblies screen restored for them; products link or copy them; Clone keeps them linked | Done |
| 2026-09-15 | Grid lines both ways on every grid; grids with height to spare padded with empty rows (`GridFillerRows`) | Done |
| 2026-09-16 | Linked ingredients: a third source costed as a share of another ingredient's cost (egg white and yolk from the egg), the share taken of the unbuffered cost with the linked-to ingredient's buffer percent inherited on top, the parts of one ingredient capped at 100% of its weight and cost, links in the cost graph and its cycle checks; the Raw Materials screen's Linked Cost card | Done |
| 2026-09-16 | Two crashes fixed in `GridFillerRows` (a stale current cell moved back to, filler rows left overflowing a grouped list after an unannounced expand), found from the event log and reproduced by fuzzing a real grid; `OffscreenGrid` grid tests | Done |

| 2026-09-17 | Category picker's Add New row renames itself to match the typed text, at the user's request, so it survives ComboBoxAdv's own AutoComplete filtering instead of "No Records Found" | Done |
| 2026-09-17 | New Category prompt pre-fills what was already typed, and its Cancel button — previously wired to nothing, so it silently did nothing — now closes the dialog | Done |

| 2026-09-17 | Fixed: switching Products' Recipe Structure tabs left the percentage column with the previous tab's header (Baker's % over absolute figures, or the reverse); the header is a `HeaderTemplate` bound to the view's DataContext instead of a bound `HeaderText` | Done |
| 2026-09-17 | Sub-Assemblies list: only the Name and Role columns, at the user's request (Batch (g), Batch Cost and Used By removed; the form shows them). `StandaloneSubAssemblyRow` keeps those properties, which the refresh merge compares to spot a changed row | Done |
| 2026-09-18 | Packaging through Add Sub-Assembly, at the user's request: a new product's Recipe Structure starts empty; the Packaging role (new), or a packaging set or another product's packaging (link or copy) fills the Packaging tab, which only shows while there's packaging; the link is saved with Save (`PackagingLinkDraft`), Make a Copy unlinks, Delete Sub-Assembly removes it; the Links & Clone card is gone (the tab is Clone) | Done |
| 2026-09-18 | Packaging sets, at the user's request: a seeded, flagged Packaging role (Id 6); a standalone sub-assembly in it holds packaging materials per set on the Sub-Assemblies screen (the Packaging tab's columns); a product's packaging can link one, one set per piece; migration `PackagingSubAssemblies` | Done |
| 2026-09-18 | Several sub-assemblies in one role, at the user's request, reversing 2026-09-17's one in each role: a product's own has an optional name again, and sections in a role are told apart by name (at most one without), their tabs reading "Filling: Ube" beside "Filling: Cheese"; a role or name change is refused where a linking product couldn't tell its sections apart; no migration | Done |
| 2026-09-18 | Material categories are typed Ingredient or Packaging, at the user's request: a Type column and picker in Settings → Material Categories; a material only goes in a category of its own type, and Raw Materials' Category picker lists only those; a category's type is fixed while materials are in it; migration `RawMaterialCategoryType` | Done |
| 2026-09-19 | Current Price and Markup % in the Products header, at the user's request: the price the shop charges now (per piece, VAT included) is entered there and saved on the product, and Markup % shows what it earns on the base cost with VAT taken out (`CostingRules.CalculateMarkupPercentFromPrice`); migration `ProductCurrentPrice` | Done |
| 2026-09-19 | Fixed buffered cost, at the user's request: a purchased material's `BufferedCostPerBaseUnit` no longer follows brand prices; it's worked out on the first price, typed on Purchase Cost Setup (Buffered Cost per base unit), or re-initialised by Recalculate (dearest brand × (1 + Cost Buffer %)), with a warning when the dearest brand costs more; linked parts take their share of it; no migration | Done |
| 2026-09-19 | Buffered Cost typed and shown in a picked unit (base or alternative), to 2 decimals, kept unrounded per base unit; migration `RawMaterialCostPrecision` takes costs per base unit to `decimal(28,20)` | Done |
| 2026-09-19 | The Raw Materials header's Current Cost and Buffered Cost tiles show the saved costs in the Purchase Cost Setup's Per unit (e.g. per bag, to 2 decimals; per gram to 4), at the user's request | Done |
| 2026-09-19 | Purchase Cost Setup: typing the Cost Buffer % works out the Buffered Cost (dearest brand × (1 + %)), typing the Buffered Cost works out the % over the dearest brand; the grid's Buffered (₱) column became Buffer %, the buffered cost over each brand's own price, at the user's request | Done |
| 2026-09-19 | Raw Materials header: the Name box moved from the tiles to the first field of the Type / Category / Source row, at the user's request | Done |
| 2026-09-19 | Raw Materials: cards 4px apart (was 16px) between the list and the details, under the summary header, and between the Alternative Units cards, at the user's request; the header card's own height unchanged | Done |
| 2026-09-19 | Sub-Assemblies, Products and Reports follow Raw Materials' spacing, at the user's request: 4px page margin and 4px between cards (were 16px, 12px and 16px) | Done |
| 2026-09-19 | Raw Materials: the details tabs wrapped in a rounded card (`CardStyle`, 4px padding), at the user's request | Done |
| 2026-09-19 | Raw Materials: the cards inside the tabs lost their own frames (plain 12px padding), so each tab's content sits straight in the tab card, at the user's request | Done |
| 2026-09-19 | Raw Materials: the details `TabControlExt`'s own border removed (`BorderThickness="0"`), at the user's request | Done |
| 2026-09-19 | Products follows Raw Materials' layout: the header card, then the tabs in a card of their own 4px below, the tab control without a border, at the user's request | Done |
| 2026-09-19 | Sub-Assemblies and Products list cards as wide as Raw Materials': Name 220px, Category/Role 185px, 16px padding, `Auto` page column, at the user's request | Done |
| 2026-09-19 | Products Recipe Structure: each tab's title (its role, e.g. Dough, or Packaging) removed as the tab header already names it; Cost per piece moved onto the Remove Line row, at the user's request | Done |
| 2026-09-20 | Quantity Required replaces Packs, at the user's request ("first let's have the product a quantity required. then we can set the necessary packaging in the subassembly"): `Product.QuantityRequired` says how many pieces make one sellable unit, every cost and price on the Products screen is for one whole unit, and a "Box of 8" is a Create Variant with a quantity of 8 and a packaging set of its own. `ProductPack`, the Packs tab and `PackagingLine.PackId` are gone; migration `ProductQuantityRequiredReplacesPacks` | Done |
| 2026-09-20 | Add Sub-Assembly is a Syncfusion `DropDownButtonAdv`, at the user's request: its three items (new, link an existing one, copy an existing one) pick the way in before the form opens, so the form's own **Sub-Assembly** picker is gone — its title names the way instead, and the source picker has the row to itself. `AddUsageCommand` carries a `UsageFormMode`, and the button stays live on the New Sub-Assembly tab, where another item changes an untouched form in place; no migration | Done |
| 2026-09-20 | Weight per Piece and Yield are no longer required to put a sub-assembly in a product's recipe or to save it, at the user's request: an unsized section (`AuthoredValue` 0) keeps its batch and costs nothing per piece until one figure is typed, which the form's caption says; `ProductRules.EnsureValidUsages` and the table's check constraint now refuse only a negative; migration `OptionalUsagePieceSize` | Done |
| 2026-09-20 | Packaging quantities are entered in any of the material's units, at the user's request ("I have an idea to utilize the alternative units stored per packaging materials"): the Unit column of all three packaging grids is a picker of that row's material's base unit and alternative units, a read-only **Base Qty** beside it shows what the quantity comes to in base units, and the line stores the unit it was typed in. `SubAssemblyPackagingLine` gained `UnitId` (migration `SetPackagingLineUnits`), and the Products grids finally write `PackagingLine.UnitId`, which had been in the schema unused since 2026-09-17 | Done |
| 2026-09-20 | The Unit picker is a `GridMultiColumnDropDownList`, at the user's request ("what i had in mind is using multi column dropdown"), replacing the `GridComboBoxColumn` of earlier the same day: its popup shows Cost per Unit then Unit, matching the control the Material cell and Purchase Cost Setup already use. **Unit Cost now shows what one of the picked unit costs** ("the unit cost column is cost per unit if the unit is converted to the alternative unit"), with the row keeping a private cost-per-base-unit for the line total so it can't double-convert. Qty stays its own column: the control commits one matched item, so typed quantity can't live in the same cell. No migration | Done |
| 2026-09-20 | The Unit picker settles as an **editable `GridComboBoxColumn`** and **Base Qty is dropped**, at the user's request ("we can convert back the unit column's dropdown to editable ComboBoxAdv having the raw material's unit … we can also drop the Base Qty column … the units cost for alternative unit will be converted behind the scenes"), after two `GridMultiColumnDropDownList` shapes were tried and reverted. **Unit Cost showing what one of the picked unit costs stays** — that part the user kept. The conversion to base units is no longer shown anywhere: `PackagingLineRow.BaseQty` feeds the line total, and `BaseQtyText` went with the column. No migration | Done |
| 2026-09-21 | Purchase Cost Setup: the Cost Buffer %, Buffered Cost and Per row moved below the brand-price grid, Recalculate removed (typing the % again re-applies it), and the % box capped at 0–100, all at the user's request | Done |
| 2026-09-21 | The Cost Buffer % follows the dearest brand price (the buffered cost stays fixed), at the user's request: the box is the live ratio of the amount over the dearest brand, clamped to 0-100, so it agrees with the grid's Buffer % column; typing the % still sets the amount | Done |
| 2026-09-21 | Made ingredients: no cost buffer of their own (their components are already buffered), so Butter Icing's buffered cost fell from ₱0.4374 to ₱0.2372/g and the three stale percents were normalised to 0; the Raw Materials header gained Total Weight (the yield) and Total Cost (the BOM at its components' buffered costs) for a made ingredient, with its Buffered Cost per g tile following the form so the three agree while typing; a new made ingredient defaults to 3% weight loss | Done |
| 2026-09-21 | Raw materials carry two dates, at the user's request: `UpdatedAt` (when the form last saved it) and `CostUpdatedAt` (when its stored cost last moved, stamped by the DbContext from the change tracker so every cost path counts), the Last Updated tile shows the cost date alone, to the day; migration `RawMaterialUpdatedDates`, existing rows backfilled | Done |
| 2026-09-21 | The Raw Materials header matches the Products header's measurements, at the user's request: 12px card padding, a 40px avatar, a 20px title, 14px SemiBold tile values, and no bordered box around the tiles (a plain row with separators, as Products' figures are) — about 275px tall to about 215px | Done |
| 2026-09-21 | Add Sub-Assembly's drop-down items are flat, at the user's request ("why do the buttons in the Add Sub-Assembly not flat?"): `Background="Transparent"` and `BorderThickness="0"` on the three `ButtonAdv`s, which the Windows11Light template `TemplateBinding`s, so they read as menu rows instead of raised buttons. The theme's own hover still repaints the row but is a 3-level grey step; a stronger one would need a template override. No migration | Done |
| 2026-09-22 | The navigation menu floats: hidden until the header's hamburger summons it, sliding in from the left over the tabs and closing on a click away, at the user's request ("can the navigation drawer be made floating?"). `SfNavigationDrawer` moved from `DisplayMode="Compact"` to `Default`, whose template draws no toggle and ignores `Items`/`FooterItems`, so the toggle (a flat icon-only `ButtonAdv` in the header row, outside the drawer's scrim) and the menu (a templated `ListBox` in `DrawerContentView`, Settings a `ButtonAdv` below it) are hand-rolled; `DrawerWidth` is live at last (240 — it was inert under Compact, which used `ExpandedModeWidth`'s 250); arrows walk the rows (by index — a ListBox's TabNavigation="Once" makes MoveFocus skip them), Enter opens, Escape closes; focus shows as the hover wash, never an outline. The menu marks nothing as current — that only repeated the tab strip — so it navigates on the row's click rather than on the `ListBox`'s selection. A top-sliding variant (`Position="Top"`) and a `MenuAdv` menu bar were both tried or costed the same day and dropped — see the `SfNavigationDrawer` bullet. No migration | Done |
| 2026-09-22 | No currency symbol on any amount shown, at the user's request ("Don't show currency symbol in all monetary value"): every money `StringFormat` in the views is now `'{}{0:N4}'`/`'{}{0:N2}'`, the ViewModels' money texts (`PurchaseCostText`, `CurrentCostText`, `BufferedCostText`, `LinkedCostText`, `UsageFormCostPerPieceText` and the rest) interpolate the figure alone, Current Price's `CurrencyTextBox` carries `CurrencySymbol=""`, and the Purchase Cost Setup's "Purchase Price (₱)" and "Buffered Cost (₱)" labels dropped the symbol. The figures themselves are unchanged; only what's printed in front of them. |
| 2026-09-22 | Two decimals on a product’s costing figures, at the user's request ("Material Cost, Base Cost and the value of Labor Cost, Overhead Cost, Administrative Cost, Vat, Mark-up should be displayed in a value with two decimal places"): `ProductHeaderCard`’s Material Cost and Base Cost tiles and `CostingCard`’s Labor, Overhead, Administrative, Markup, VAT and Subtotal (base cost) went from `N4` to `N2`, in the tile captions and the summary table alike. Sub-assemblies and Packaging followed the same day, on the second half of the request, in the table and in the Packaging section’s header. Display only — the cascade still computes at full precision, and the recipe tabs’ Cost per piece still shows 4. |
| 2026-09-22 | Shrinkage % on each Recipe Structure section, at the user's request ("Add Shrinkage % on top of the Weight per Piece (g) in the Recipe Structure. Final Weight (g) = Total Weight (g) * (1 - Shrinkage %)"): `ProductSubAssemblyUsage.ShrinkagePercent` (0 to under 100, `CK_ProductSubAssemblyUsages_ShrinkagePercent`, migration `UsageShrinkagePercent`), `ProductRules.CalculateFinalWeight`, and `ResolvePieceSize`/`CalculateUsageCostPerPiece` now dividing the final weight rather than the total. The user chose to have it drive the yield rather than sit there as a figure: the weight per piece is the finished piece, and a batch that loses weight makes fewer, dearer pieces. Existing usages carry over at 0%, so nothing reprices. The form gained a Shrinkage % box above Weight per Piece and a read-only Final Weight (g) under Total Weight | Done |
| 2026-09-22 | The Recipe Structure form's pickers filter as you type, at the user's request ("the combobox for selecting Existing Sub-Assembly or Packaging is not searchable"): Existing Sub-Assembly or Packaging and Role set `IsEditable`/`AutoCompleteMode` in XAML, because `ApplyPickerDefaults` never sees a picker inside a panel that is collapsed when the screen loads (see the Syncfusion UI section). |
| 2026-09-23 | Sub-Assemblies form: Used By shows the linking products as tiles in rows and columns, at the user's request, instead of a sentence naming them; then, also at the user's request, long names wrap, and each tile has an icon, like a file in File Explorer, and opens its product on the Products screen when clicked | Done |
| 2026-09-23 | Used By tiles show what each product is, at the user's request ("find suitable icons (with colors) for cakes, breads, and pastries"): `CakeIcon`, `PastryIcon` and `BreadIcon` in `App.xaml` are flat full-color `DrawingImage`s on a 32×32 canvas (a two-tier pink-iced cake with a cherry, a croissant, a slice of loaf bread; the croissant gave way to the downloaded cupcake image on 2026-10-08), picked by `ProductCategoryIconConverter` from the product's category name, since categories are names users type: keywords in English and Filipino (cake, chiffon, mamon; pastr, croissant, pie, tart, hopia; bread, loaf, bun, tinapay, pandesal, ensaymada), checked cake, then pastry, then bread, with short keywords matched only as whole words or word starts. Any other product keeps the Products glyph. `LinkingProduct` gained `CategoryName`. No migration | Done |
| 2026-09-23 | Products: a linked standalone sub-assembly's badge opens it on the Sub-Assemblies screen, at the user's request ("the caption for the link subassembly be clickable"), the reverse of the Used By tiles. The recipe section's "Chocolate Glaze, from Sub-Assemblies" badge and the Packaging tab's "Linked to Gift Box Set (Sub-Assemblies)" badge are `LinkedBadgeLinkStyle` buttons (the badge's look, underlined under the mouse) running `OpenSubAssemblyCommand`; `MainWindow` subscribes to `ProductsViewModel.SubAssemblyOpenRequests` and calls `SubAssembliesViewModel.OpenSubAssembly`, which, like `OpenProduct`, clears a search hiding the row or leaves the id for the next load, and the list scrolls to it. A badge naming another product ("From Cream Loaf") stays plain. No migration | Done |
| 2026-09-23 | Back and Forward between screens, at the user's request ("previous/next view so that i can navigate back and forth easily between views"): header buttons and Alt+Left / Alt+Right. A step is a change of screen, and Back reopens the record that screen had open when it was left, unless its form has unsaved changes (see Application Architecture, "A tab keeps its view state"). `NavigationHistory`, `RawMaterialsViewModel.OpenRawMaterial`. No migration | Done |
| 2026-09-23 | Every sub-assembly on the Sub-Assemblies screen, at the user's request: a section a product makes is saved there on Save, named "<Product> <Role>" (": <name>" when it has one, numbered when taken), and the product links it; its lines are edited only there; removing a section or deleting the product keeps it; Clone links like Create Variant; a linked section saved from the product goes by the rest of its name. `OwnerProductId` dropped (migration `SubAssembliesAllStandalone`), after a one-off script converted the 11 owned ones in the dev database | Done |
| 2026-09-23 | Product-owned roles, at the user's request: Settings → Roles' Product-owned checkbox (`SubAssemblyRole.IsProductOwned`, not the Packaging role); a section made in a product in such a role is that product's own (edited in its recipe, off the Sub-Assemblies screen, copied not linked by others, deleted with it or its product, renamed with it or its role); turning a role on adopts the ones only one product uses (add-ins merged), off releases them; Clone and Variant copy owned sections. Migration `ProductOwnedRoles`; the dev database's Filling, Topping and Pan Greasing ticked in the app (7 adopted, Milk Streusel kept) | Done |
| 2026-09-23 | Unlink on a linked recipe section, at the user's request: a copy that is the product's own in any role (`NewSubAssemblyDraft.IsOwn`), beside Make a Copy, which is unchanged; an own section is released only on leaving a product-owned role; a role rename renames own ones in any role. No migration | Done |
| 2026-09-23 | Undoing an Unlink or a Make a Copy no longer leaves Save offered, at the user's request: `UndoHistory` keeps the settled snapshot beside each step, and the Products restore rebases its form part back (`WithFormOf`). No migration | Done |
| 2026-09-23 | Move to Sub-Assemblies, Unlink's reverse, at the user's request: a product's own section, in any role, goes to the Sub-Assemblies screen on Save keeping its name and lines, and the product links it (`NewSubAssemblyDraft.Release`). No migration | Done |
| 2026-09-24 | Raw Materials: a Used By tab, at the user's request ("determines which product is using the raw material similar to Sub-Assemblies"), between Usage Example and Price History (`PriceHistoryTab` is now 4). `IRawMaterialRepository.GetUsedByAsync` is one recursive query: an ingredient counts through sub-assembly lines and add-ins, and so does every made ingredient it's in and linked ingredient cut from it; packaging counts through a product's own lines (ignored while its packaging links), a linked packaging set, and every product linking that product's packaging down the chain. The tiles open their product on Products, as Sub-Assemblies' do; the tile style and list moved to `App.xaml` (`UsedByTileStyle`, `UsedByTilesStyle`) so both screens share them. No migration | Done |
| 2026-09-25 | Header tiles centred, at the user's request: each figure's label and value sit in the middle of its share of the row on the Raw Materials (`SummaryHeaderCard`), Products (`ProductHeaderCard`) and Sub-Assemblies headers (`TextAlignment="Center"` on their label and value styles). A packaging material's Base Unit picker still fills its tile, as input boxes do everywhere else | Done |
| 2026-09-25 | The Products Recipe Structure's Cost/piece and Batch cost tiles centred too, at the user's request (`StatCaptionStyle` and `StatValueStyle` in `UsageFormPanel`, `TextAlignment="Center"`), as the headers' tiles are | Done |
| 2026-09-25 | The Recipe Structure's Cost/piece tile shows two decimals, as Batch cost beside it does, at the user's request (`ProductsViewModel.RecomputeUsageForm`, `N4` to `N2`). Only the display: the cost per piece is kept at full precision. The packaging form's "Cost per piece" footer keeps four | Done |
| 2026-09-25 | The Costing Summary breaks the Sub-assemblies (ingredients) row down, at the user's request ("Add breakdown of cost for Sub-Assemblies (ingredients) in Costing Summary"): under the row, one line per sub-assembly in recipe order, called by its recipe tab's name (`SectionName`, e.g. "Dough" or "Filling: Ube"), with its cost per sellable unit (cost per piece × Quantity Required) and its share of the price. Indented, 12px and muted so the lines read as parts of the total rather than more rows to add; `SharedSizeGroup`s line their figures up with the table's columns. Display only: `ProductCostSummary.SubAssemblyLines`, from the same usages that make the total. |

The tile now shows only the saved base unit, "name (abbr)", or "—" until the material's first Save (`RawMaterialsViewModel.SavedBaseUnitText`); an ingredient's still reads gram (g) | Done |
| 2026-09-26 | The Raw Materials and Products Category pickers lose their "< Add New .. >" row, at the user's request: a name typed into either that isn't a category brings up a prompt to add it once the picker is left or Enter is pressed (`Controls/CategoryTextCommit`, `ICategoryPickerScreen`), and a listed name is picked whatever its case. Also fixed: a grid cell refused by `GridCellEditing` left `GridUndoSteps` marking an edit pending, which kept Save offered with nothing changed (found checking this in the app; the Purchase Cost Setup's locked Qty cell did it too) | Done |
| 2026-09-26 | The Products list becomes a tree of folders, at the user's request: Production and Research and Development (`Product.Stage`, migration `ProductStage`; the products already there go into Production, a new one into Research and Development), a folder per product category in each, and a product dragged onto a folder moved there at once (`IProductRepository.MoveAsync`) | Done |

| 2026-09-26 | The Products tree is white like the Raw Materials and Sub-Assemblies grids, at the user's request: a Border behind it filled with the new `ListBackgroundBrush`, since the theme draws the tree transparent and ignores its `Background` | Done |
| 2026-09-26 | The Products tree's right-click menus, at the user's request: Promote / Send Back / Move to, Rename, Clone, Create Variant and Delete on a product; New Product Here, Promote All and the category's New / Rename / Delete on a folder; Expand All and Collapse All; several products moved at once; F2, Del and Enter; folder counts; a notice with Undo after each move; and the open folders remembered between runs (`IViewStateStore`). `MoveAsync` takes several moves, and `RenameAsync` and `GetByIdsAsync` are new; no schema change | Done |

| 2026-10-03 | Database backup and restore, at the user's request: Settings → **Database** (`DatabaseBackupsView`/`DatabaseBackupsViewModel`) with Back Up Now, Copy to…, Restore and Restore from File…, and the day's automatic backup on the first start of each day (`App.BackUpDailyAsync`), the newest 14 automatic ones kept. `IDatabaseBackups`/`DatabaseBackups` run SQL Server's own `BACKUP … COPY_ONLY, CHECKSUM` and `RESTORE … REPLACE` into `%ProgramData%\TinapayanRMS\Backups`, which the app creates and, as its owner, gives the SQL service's account and Users Modify on (`BackupFolder`): the service can't write to a user's profile. A restore checks the file, takes a safety backup, restores with the files moved onto the database's own, and brings a backup from an earlier version up to date with `MigrateAsync` (the user's choice; a later version's, or another database's, is refused, `BackupRules.UpdatesNeeded`); anything failing after the safety backup puts it back. The app restarts after a restore (`App.RestartAfterExit`), since every singleton ViewModel holds the old data. `Failure` maps SQL Server's backup errors by any error in the exception (3013 follows the real one; 3254 is a short file that isn't a backup, 3241 a longer one). Tests restore over a `ThrowawayDatabase` of their own; no schema change | Done |
| 2026-10-03 | **What-If Prices**, a screen of its own, at the user's request (`WhatIfView`/`WhatIfViewModel`, `:whatif`): a table of price changes (a purchased material, its dearest brand's unit and price, Change % or New Price, each worked out from the other, `WhatIfPriceChange`, `PurchaseUnit`) and every product's Base Cost, Suggested Price and Markup %, now and what-if, with Only affected products and Export to Excel. Session-only, never saved, no "apply". A change moves the material's true and buffered costs by the same factor (`PriceChangeRules.CostsAfter`; a real price change leaves the fixed buffered cost alone, so this is the price change with the buffer % typed again). It reaches made and linked ingredients through `IngredientCostRules.Reprice`, the cascade's own per-ingredient math (`IngredientCostRules.Recompute`, moved out of `IngredientCostPropagator`, which now calls it), only dependents recomputed, so an untouched product prices exactly as Reports does; products are priced by `SavedProductReader.Build` with substitute materials, and composites by `SavedProductReader.PricedAt`. `IRawMaterialBrandPriceRepository.GetAllAsync`. The export writes a picker column's shown name, not its id. A row raises its change only once its own state has settled: an event raised from inside `RaiseAndSetIfChanged` reached the recalculation before the setter had marked which figure was typed. Per and Current Price take no focus (`AllowFocus="False"`), so Tab goes from the material picked to Change %: with them focusable, Tab left the add row and committed it, found driving the screen in the app; no schema change | Done |
| 2026-10-03 | **Production Plan**, a screen of its own, at the user's request (`ProductionPlanView`/`ProductionPlanViewModel`, `:plan`): one plan, kept per Windows user in the view-state file (`production-plan`, `"id:units"`), the user's choice over saved plans; the products and sellable units to make, then a **Mixing Sheet** (each mix in whole batches, the products it's for, the made ingredients to prepare) and a **Purchase Requisition** (each material's total in its base unit, the whole purchase units to buy in its dearest brand's unit, rounded up, the user's choice, and its cost at buffered costs), Print and Export to Excel. The arithmetic is pure, `ProductionPlanRules.Plan`: a section's unrounded batches (`RecipeScalingRules.BatchRatio`, split out of `BatchesFor`) are added up per sub-assembly and set of add-ins (add-ins go in every batch, so a use with its own is a mix of its own) and rounded up once (`WholeBatches`), so products sharing a dough share its batches; made ingredients are opened into their BOMs (`ScaleBom`), and a linked ingredient becomes what it's cut from (grams ÷ weight share), the largest of one ingredient's parts deciding, its own direct use on top. `WholeBatches` and `UnitsToBuy` never round a real need down to nothing (1 g in 25 kg sacks is a sack; a tiny ratio is a batch), which also stops the Scaling tab calling a tiny section unsized. `ProductionPlanBuilder` maps saved products to the rule (through `ProductionPlanSource`, built once per load since 2026-10-24). The mixing sheet is a flat table, not the Scaling tab's tree, so it exports as it is. Printing goes through `ProductPrintLayout` (`ProductPrintSet.Plan`, `AddPlan`) in a `PrintPreviewWindow` dialog that themes itself before the pane goes in; no schema change | Done |
| 2026-10-06 | What-If Prices: **Print Report**, at the user's request: each product's recipe and costing at the what-if prices beside today's, for the products picked in the table (Ctrl/Shift, `SelectionMode="Extended"`, `SelectedProducts` picked again by id after every recalculation) or all those shown (the grid's `View.Records`, so its filters and sort), chosen in `PrintPreviewWindow`'s new Print dropdown (`PrintChoice`), which also gained Recipe and Costing for products. The user's choices: a print preview (paper or PDF) rather than Excel; the costing as Now, What-If and Change columns; moved lines marked in the accent with a Cost Now column; a Price Changes Applied table per product. `WhatIfReport` pairs the product priced now and what-if by index (one `ProductDetail` builds both), setting `NowCost`/`NowBatchCost` where a figure moves (exact `!=`) and `PrintedWhatIf`; a change reaches a product when `IngredientCostRules.ReachedBy` meets `WhatIfReport.MaterialIdsOf`. The first page lists every change and each product's base cost now and what-if (`ProductPrintSet.PriceChanges`). Without the costing it prints as a plain set. The report is built from the last recalculation's results, so it matches the table even while a refused change waits; no schema change | Done |

| 2026-11-04 | **A small cleanup from the same code review** (at the user's request). **A recursive query that meets a loop in the data now stops**, where it ran until the command timed out after 30 seconds. The app refuses each kind of loop, but one made outside it (a hand edit) left the Raw Materials screen's Used By waiting 30 seconds for an error, and failed a Save whose products' packaging links ran into one (`ProductRepository.GetProductsReadingAsync`). `RawMaterialRepository.UsedBySql`'s `feeds`, `recipe_users` and `packaging_costs`, and `GetProductsReadingAsync`'s `chain`, carry a `CYCLE` clause; `packaging_users` can't meet a loop. `UNION` would have ended them too, but it made Used By 19 to 26 ms slower a read, from a hash table PostgreSQL sized by a row estimate thousands of times too high, where `CYCLE` costs about a millisecond. Four tests put a loop in past the repositories, and each timed out before the change. On the dev database every material's Used By and every product's and sub-assembly's readers came out byte-identical (334 answers). **The code nothing called went**, with its tests: `RecipeScalingRules.HalfBatch`, `HalfBatches`, `BatchesToMake` and `UnitsToBuy`, `ProductRules.CalculateLineGramsPerPiece` and `CalculateLineCostPerPiece` (kept until now for a per-line view), `CostingRules.CalculateProfitMarginPercent`, `GridAutoRowHeight`, and `PrintPreviewWindow`'s one-printout constructor, most of it the Production Plan's. Notes that still spoke of the Production Plan as this app's, PLAN.md's .NET line and the Recipe API's endpoint list were brought up to date. The MCP server's start was timed at about 3 s, 6 after a change and 15 from nothing, and judged in time, but none of those starts followed a commit: it still missed the wait at every session start (the next row). See PLAN.md, Conventions ("Reads through Dapper, writes through EF Core"). |
| 2026-11-04 | **The MCP server connects when a session starts** (at the user's request, from the same code review). Claude Code's logs showed 14 starts since 2026-10-31, and none connected: 13 ran past its 30-second wait and one closed. The commit was why. The SDK put each commit's hash into every assembly's version and Source Link file, so the first build after any commit, even one that changed only docs, recompiled all five projects the server runs on: 27 s with no compiler running yet, and longer beside the other servers a session starts. `Directory.Build.props` now leaves the commit out of every build but `dotnet publish`'s (`EnableSourceControlManagerQueries` and `EnableSourceLink` off unless `_IsPublishing`), so a published copy still says which commit it was built from. In a throwaway clone, an empty commit recompiled all five projects before the change and none after (a 1.4 s build), and a publish still stamped `1.0.0+<hash>` into every assembly. A commit no longer recompiles the solution for `dotnet build` and `dotnet test` either. `.claude/settings.json` raises Claude Code's wait to 2 minutes (`MCP_TIMEOUT`), for a start after a real change. Its log showed the 2 minutes in use at the next session start, which took 44 s, as all five recompiled again: the build writes each project's folder, drive letter's case and all, into a file the compiler reads, and Claude Code starts the server from `c:\…`, where the timings had been built from `C:\…` (each change of case recompiled, checked with the build servers shut down between builds). Not fixed: while one session's server runs, another session's start that has to rebuild fails, as the running server holds the files it would replace (MSB3026, checked). See PLAN.md, Tech Stack (MCP server, Build settings), and the developer manual's "Checking costs from an AI assistant". |
| 2026-11-04 | **The MCP server starts while another session's runs** (at the user's request). A running server held its files in `bin/Release/net11.0`, so another session's start that had to rebuild them failed after 13 s of retries (MSB3026). `.mcp.json` now runs `scripts/Start-McpServer.ps1` in Windows PowerShell, which builds Release, copies the build to `bin/Release/runs/<hash>`, named for a hash of each file's path, size and time, and runs the copy on its own standard input and output, so a running server holds only its copy. A start with nothing new runs the copy already there, and a copy no server runs from (Windows won't open its exe for writing while one does) goes at the next start. One start builds and copies at a time (a named mutex), and the server is in a job object that ends it with the script, as killing `dotnet run` ended its server. The build's output goes to standard error, and a build that fails stops the start rather than run an older copy. In a throwaway clone: a start that rebuilt while another server ran answered in 4.5 s, where it had failed; the running server's copy was kept, and went at the next start once it had closed; two starts at once both answered, from one copy; a start waited for a mutex held 7 s; a build that failed stopped the start, with its errors on standard error and nothing on standard output; killing the script ended its server within 3 s, as killing `dotnet run` did (without the job object, the server ran on until its input closed); and no server was left running. A start with nothing to build answers in about 2 s. PowerShell gives the script's path a capital drive letter, so it builds from `C:\…` however Claude Code was started, and its first start recompiles every project once, as `dotnet run` had built from `c:\…`. Nothing users see. See PLAN.md, Tech Stack (MCP server), and the developer manual's "Checking costs from an AI assistant". |

| 2026-11-05 | **A sub-assembly's batch weight and cost are worked out on read, not stored** (at the user's request, from the same review). `SubAssembly.TotalWeight` and `TotalCost` were kept current by a propagator of their own (`SubAssemblyCostPropagator`, run at the end of every ingredient cascade), by each line save and by half of Recalculate Costs, but product costing never read them: it prices every section from its lines, at the buffered or the true cost. Their only readers were the Recipe API's mix `BatchWeight`, the linkable sub-assemblies' `total_weight > 0` filter and two list-row properties nothing bound, and PLAN.md said both that products read them and that they didn't. The columns went (migration `DropStoredSubAssemblyTotals`) with the propagator and `TinapayanRMSDbContext.Stored`: the API sums a mix's lines, the filter asks for a line (the same thing, since a line's grams are positive by a check constraint), and Recalculate Costs counts made and linked ingredients alone. Before the migration, all 122 stored totals in the dev database matched their lines and both filters picked the same 115 sub-assemblies; every answer of the Recipe API came out byte-identical before and after. The tests that read the stored totals read the batch from the lines now (`PersistenceTestBase.SubAssemblyTotalsAsync`). See PLAN.md, Reactive Costing Cascade. |
| 2026-11-05 | **The validators run the rules instead of copying them** (at the user's request, from the same review). The Raw Materials and Products screens' messages read as the save's now, and a rule that stops at its first problem says one thing where the copies could list two. See PLAN.md, Application Architecture, and the developer manual's "Where rules are enforced". |

| 2026-11-06 | **The tests run on TUnit** (at the user's request, for its speed and its modern C#). xUnit v3 is gone: TUnit.Engine 1.81.0 (on Microsoft.Testing.Platform 2.5.2, where `Microsoft.Testing.Extensions.HotReload` 2.5.2 already was) runs every test project, and `Tests.Common` takes `TUnit.Core`; AwesomeAssertions stays, so no assertion changed. `[Fact]`/`[Theory]` became `[Test]`, `[InlineData]` `[Arguments]`, `TheoryData` a `[MethodDataSource]` method of tuples (the Mcp test's array rows `Func<…>`, TUnit0046), `Assert.SkipWhen` `Skip.When`, `TestContext.Current.CancellationToken` `.Execution.CancellationToken`. Each `[Collection(DatabaseCollection.X)]` became `[NotInParallel(X)]` plus `[ClassDataSource<DatabaseFixture>(Shared = SharedType.Keyed, Key = X)]`: one fixture, so one database, per key, keys running side by side and a key's tests one at a time, as before. `DatabaseFixtureBase` is an `IAsyncInitializer`, `PersistenceTestBase` resets in a `[Before(Test)]` hook (a probe checked it empties a database the last run left rows in, from the other assembly), and the backup tests make their throwaway database in one and drop it in `DisposeAsync`. A class's own tests now run in parallel. `tests/TestRunSettings.cs`, a `[Before(TestDiscovery)]` hook compiled into every test project, caps the tests running at once at `Environment.ProcessorCount` (TUnit's default is twice that), and turns off TUnit's HTML and JSON report, on by default, which wrote 20 MB for Infrastructure.Tests each run and recorded every Npgsql command as a span. The scripts' `-Class`/`-Method` become a `--treenode-filter`, `Test-Changed.ps1` passing `--ignore-exit-code 8` so a project the classes aren't in passes, and `Watch-Tests.ps1` now takes both at once. Hot reload reruns a changed test, but every run after the first also reported "Unhandled exception - ChannelClosedException: The channel has been closed." and ended "Failed!": the platform's hot reload (`HotReloadTestHostTestFrameworkInvoker`) runs the same request again in the same session, and TUnit 1.81.0 keeps a `DynamicTestQueue` per session, which `TestScheduler` completes at the end of every run with `Writer.Complete()`, throwing the second time. `tests/HotReloadReruns.cs` (at the user's request, a workaround), in every test project, gives that queue a new channel from a `[BeforeEvery(Test)]` hook when the run before completed it, found through `TestContext`'s internal `ServiceProvider` and the queue's private fields by name, and only when `TESTINGPLATFORM_HOTRELOAD_ENABLED` is 1. Safe because the scheduler reads the queue once as a run starts, and a completed, empty channel ends that read at once. Under `Watch-Tests.ps1`, an ApplicationCore class and a Pricing database class each passed, failed only the assertion broken on a save, and passed once it was put back, with no failure of TUnit's own. Measured on this 12-processor PC, the full suite (2201 tests) as `dotnet test --solution` runs it: xUnit 5m22s and 5m45s; TUnit at its default of 24 at once 5m43s, 6m02s, 6m35s and 6m08s; TUnit at 12 4m50s, 4m29s and 4m51s, at 6 4m36s. The suite's time is two chains that run one test at a time whatever the framework, Infrastructure's collection A, `DatabaseBackupsTests` (26 tests, 327 s, each migrating a throwaway database), and UI's off-screen grid tests (296 s), and 24 tests at once beside them in each of the six projects slowed them down. Alone, the 483 ApplicationCore tests run in 0.84–0.88 s against xUnit's 1.0–1.1 s. In nine full runs one test failed once, in the last run at 24: Respawn's reset of the UI database got PostgreSQL's 58P01, "could not extend file … No such file or directory", a server-side file error under that load, never seen at 12 or under xUnit. |

| 2026-11-08 | **The cost graph is turned round and walked once however many materials changed** (from the code review; "fix each issues from 1 to 7"). `IngredientCostGraph.GetDependentsInOrder` built the graph's dependents map and sorted the result for one changed ingredient, and every caller with several called it once each: the propagator for each ingredient a save changed (Re-apply Target Buffers on N materials built the map N times, then threw each sorted list away and sorted the union again), `IngredientCostRules.Reprice` for each simulated price, and `ReachedBy` built the whole dependency list too, once per price change on every recalculation of the Price Simulator, once per purchased material in Reports' Cost Drivers, and once per change in the MCP server's `simulate_price_changes`. Now `DependentsOf` turns the graph round, `GetDependents` walks it from many ingredients together, `GetDependentsInOrder` takes many, and `ReachedBy` takes a list of materials and builds the graph once; the propagator, `Reprice`, the Price Simulator, `CostDriverAnalysis` and `CostingTools` use them. | 2026-11-08 | **A save PostgreSQL cancels for one beside it is tried again** (from the code review; "fix each issues from 1 to 7"). A unit of work runs Serializable (`SharedTransactionDbContextFactory.RunAsync`), and PostgreSQL ends such a transaction when another beside it got there first: "could not serialize access" (40001) or a deadlock (40P01). Nothing tried it again, so two saves at once (two screens, or two copies of the app) could show the user an error for a save that only needed doing again. **`RunAsync` now runs the work again from the start**, on a new connection, up to three times (`Attempts`), 10 to 50 ms times the try apart at random, when the failure is one of those two, however EF Core and Npgsql wrap it (`IsCancelledForAnother`); a run inside a run leaves it to the outer one, and a rule's refusal isn't tried again. Every run's work is `VersionedSaves`', repository calls only, which copy the entities they're given, so a try that was rolled back leaves nothing behind. Tests: `A_run_cancelled_for_a_save_beside_it_is_run_again_and_sees_what_that_save_committed` (a rename committed between the run's read and its write gives 40001, and the second try reads it), `A_run_cancelled_every_time_gives_up_after_its_attempts_with_what_postgresql_said`, `A_run_refused_by_a_rule_isnt_run_again` | Done |
| 2026-11-08 | **A buffer percent changed on its own reaches the ingredients linked to it** (from the code review; "fix each issues from 1 to 7"). `RawMaterialRepository.UpdateAsync` cascaded an ingredient's buffer when it changed, but for a purchased one the flag was overwritten by whether its buffered cost changed: a new percent with the same buffered cost (no price yet, so 0 at any percent, or a buffered cost sent again as it was) didn't cascade, and its linked parts, which copy its percent, went on showing the old one until something else moved its cost. Either change cascades now (`bufferChanged |=`). Test: `A_buffer_percent_changed_on_the_egg_reaches_its_parts_when_its_buffered_cost_stays_the_same`, checked to fail without the fix | Done |

Checked in the window: the Satori build started, logged `Garbage collector: Satori, latency mode "Interactive"`, and drew every screen, visited three times over, with no error; the everyday build is unchanged (framework-dependent, no Satori files). Its runtime is RTM while the rest of .NET is the SDK's RC3; nothing broke. No pause or memory figures yet | Done |

| 2026-11-13 | **A review of the app as a .NET MAUI Blazor Hybrid app, and its Immediate and Short-term fixes** (the user asked for a technical review covering architecture, stability, memory, the MAUI and Blazor Hybrid specifics, performance, DI, diagnostics, security, maintainability and testing, with each finding's location, severity, risk, cause and fix; then, asked, chose "Immediate + Short-term" fixes, no review page, and one copy per PC). It found nothing critical. ReactiveUI 26.0.1 and MAUI 11.0.0 were decompiled to confirm two findings. **Fixed**: **What fails with nothing to catch it is logged** (`UnhandledFailures`): an exception ending the process, a task nothing awaited, and an exception on the window's thread (WinUI's `UnhandledException`). Nothing logged them before: MAUI's `MauiWinUIApplication` hooks none, and PLAN.md said unobserved tasks reached the log. **ReactiveUI gets a handler** (`WithExceptionHandler`). Its default rethrew a failed command's exception on the calling thread through `MainThreadScheduler`: the page's error bar on the window's thread, the end of the process on any other. Now the failure is logged and shown as a toast. **A screen that fails gives way to a panel** (`ScreenErrorBoundary`), not the whole page. Its ViewModel keeps the unsaved changes, and `UnsavedChangesGuard` keeps a form whose screen went with them, so closing still asks. **Leaving waits for a Save on its way.** Yes asked meanwhile ran a second Save, since `SaveCommand.Execute()` runs whatever `CanExecute` says. The wait is on the Save's own task (`RecordFormViewModel.RunSaveAsync`): the command's `IsExecuting` is set on ReactiveUI's output scheduler, and leaning on it failed the new-product test one run in three. **The window stays open while a backup or restore runs** (`IBusyWork`), and **a restore cut short between its renames is put back at the next start** (`RecoverInterruptedRestoreAsync`). **MAUI's title bar switch, found by reflection, is guarded.** A throw there, in the window's message hook, would have ended the app on every maximize. **A page registration made after its component went is stopped** (`PageWatches.KeepUnlessGoneAsync`). The undo keys' document listener outlived its screen and blurred the field on every Ctrl+Z. **Discarded UI tasks are logged** (`TaskForgetting.Forget`), `app.js`'s calls into .NET catch their failures, and `NoticeHost` shows only the latest of two quick messages. **A failed start says why** in a message box (`StartupMessage`). **One copy on the PC** (`OneCopyPerPc`): another Windows sign-in, or the WPF app, refuses the start. A `Global\` mutex keyed by the folder wouldn't have worked, since the installer puts each user's copy in their own folder. **Corrected** in the docs: PLAN.md's Syncfusion version (35.1.46), and maui-app.md's glyphs, which are Syncfusion's, not `Glyphs.cs`'s. **Not done**, now open items below: optimistic concurrency, the Recipe API over HTTPS, CI, splitting the three largest ViewModels, runtime async in a published build, a second host before macOS or Linux, scripted smoke and soak runs, and a docs path test. **Tests**: `LeavingWhileSavingTests`, `UnhandledFailuresTests`, `OneCopyPerPcTests`, `ScreenErrorBoundaryTests`, `UnsavedChangesGuardTests`, `UndoKeysTests` and two `DatabaseBackupsTests`. It now says what holds: every click, key and call from the page's script reaches .NET on the UI thread, a screen state's awaits come back to it (nothing uses `ConfigureAwait(false)`), and its classes take no lock, so that's what keeps them whole; what does change on other threads is what the window shares with the background work (`AppStatus`, `DatabaseReadiness` and `UnhandledFailures.Reported`; `AppLock` promises no thread either), which is why a component handling their events goes through `InvokeAsync` or its `RedrawGate`. The review found the app holds to it: every `Task.Run` in the screens is handed what it reads on the UI thread first, and every load a selection drives (`LoadLatest`) checks it's still wanted before it applies anything. ReactiveUI, set up with `WithBlazor()`, ran its scheduler on the calling thread, so it never moved work to the UI thread, and its going changed none of this. |

## Open items (for later phases)

In the order they're worth doing, as reassessed on 2026-11-11.

- **ADR 0002's phase 4: the remaining screens onto use cases** (phases 1 to 3 done: Sub-Assemblies, Raw Materials and Products, phase 3's done-when amended on 2026-11-21), one at a time, each through a pull request (ADR 0005), with no feature work on a screen while it moves, on Sub-Assemblies' pattern (phase 1, in the log): an editor and queries in `UseCases/<Screen>/`, a draft with `Show`, `Capture`, `Restore`, `Rebase` (re-point only, ADR 0007) and `ToSaveRequest`, its property tests, and the screen's types out of `DataAccessTests` and `ConstructorDependencyTests`' lists. **Phase 4** Settings, Reports, the Price Simulator and Printing, and Presentation's reference to Infrastructure (`LayerTests.StillThere`), in steps by use case, each its own pull request: (1) Settings' list tabs, Costing Defaults and Printing, done 2026-11-21; (2) the password and the backups: Security, the sign-in panel, Database, the automatic backups and the database update as the app opens, done 2026-11-21; (3) Quick Find and Reports, done 2026-11-21; (4) the Price Simulator, done 2026-11-21; (5) the Print dialog, done 2026-11-21; (6) Presentation's reference to Infrastructure (`ScreenState`'s failure words, `ProductTree`'s use of a configuration, `AutomaticBackups`).
- **An undo across a load puts back the costs the load moved** (found by `ProductRecipeDraftTests`, 2026-11-21; as it has always been). A Products undo step keeps its sections with the ingredients they held then, and a load replaces a changed ingredient with a fresh copy (`CollectionMerge.MergeFrom`) and rewrites the history only for linked lines and packaging (`ProductForm.TakeLoad`), so a section an undo puts back after a load that moved its ingredients' costs shows the old costs, the header's too, until the next load. Not done in the move (ADR 0005): the restore could re-point the sections as a load does (`ProductRecipeDraft.RefreshRows` on the view's last read), or the load could rewrite every step's sections at the fresh costs.
- **A Raw Materials load can show a tab older Dependents than its own read** (from phase 2's review, there before it): the view's load reads the products using each open tab's material as it sets out. A tab switched to material Y during the load, while another tab shows Y, has its fresh tiles replaced at the load's end by Y's as the load began, and their shares worked out again. Rare, and the next load or pick puts it right; the fix is to show the catalog's copy only to a form whose details were in place before the load set out.
- **The Satori GC experiment, kept or dropped**: the Maui project imports the `PublishWithSatoriGC` SDK, a prerelease (`11.0.1-satori.…`), in every build, though it's off unless `-p:UseSatoriGC=true`. ADR 0005 allows no prerelease in a release build that isn't named with what it waits for: name it so, or take the import out until it's measured again.
- **Open record tabs restored at the next start** (left from views with their own record tabs, the user's, 2026-11-20, all five phases in the log): a record view opens on a blank new record in its preview tab each time the app starts. Keeping the tabs open (and which showed) with the view state would bring them back; ask the user first, as it wasn't asked for.
- **Todo (the user): the shop's real costs for one normal month, three if possible.** Four totals from the books: **labor** (production wages, overtime, 13th-month pay, employer contributions), **overhead** (rent, power, gas, water, repairs), **administrative** (office staff, permits, bookkeeping) and **ingredients used** (purchases, adjusted for the change in stock). Store and delivery staff go under overhead or administrative, so no cost is left out. Each percentage is that cost ÷ (ingredients used × 1.10), since products are costed at buffered prices averaging 10% above what the shop pays. The 35%, 25% and 15% in use are rules of thumb (the user, 2026-11-18), and these three make up about 40% of a typical product's base cost. With the totals: show which products move before anything is saved, then make Apply Costing Defaults leave each product's markup alone (Batch 2).
- **Tag the commit the shop runs, and see how the app feels there.** The shop's computer runs this app since 2026-11-14 (the user's word). If the commit installed there isn't tagged yet, tag it, so it can be found again: Settings → Database's **App Version** names the commit from the next install on. Then see how opening a record screen and picking a record feel on that PC, timed as on 2026-11-10 if they seem slow (on this laptop they hold the window 7 to 51 ms since the look-alike pickers of 2026-11-12).
- **Delete `tests/HotReloadReruns.cs` once TUnit's hot reload reruns stop failing without it.** It works around TUnit (1.81.0 to 1.87.12 so far) completing a session's `DynamicTestQueue` at the end of every run, which a hot reload rerun in the same session made throw. 1.86.0 still needed it (checked on 2026-11-17 by decompiling `TUnit.Engine`, whose queue and scheduler are unchanged from 1.84.0, and under `Watch-Tests.ps1`: without the file a rerun reported the ChannelClosedException, with it the reruns passed). 1.86.6's queue and scheduler decompile the same as 1.86.0's (2026-11-18, not run under `Watch-Tests.ps1`), so it still needs it, and what it reaches for is still there by the same names, so it still works. 1.87.12 still needs it (2026-11-21: its queue decompiles the same as 1.86.6's, and under `Watch-Tests.ps1` with the file switched off the first rerun reported the ChannelClosedException, with it three runs passed). On a TUnit upgrade, take it out of `tests/Directory.Build.props`, break and restore an assertion under `scripts/Watch-Tests.ps1`, and see whether a run after the first still reports "ChannelClosedException: The channel has been closed."; if it does and the file is back in, it still has to work (a renamed internal makes it do nothing). Keep `Microsoft.Testing.Extensions.HotReload` at the platform version TUnit brings.
- **From the 2026-11-13 review, not done yet, in the order it ranked them** (each finding's location, risk and fix are in that row of the log):
  - **The Recipe API over HTTPS**, if the shop's network isn't wired and trusted: its key and every cost go over plain HTTP (`http://*:5290`). A self-signed certificate the installer makes, its thumbprint pinned in TinapayanBMS; or record in PLAN.md that the network is trusted.
  - **A second host before a date for macOS or Linux**: MAUI has no Linux target, and Mac Catalyst has no window close that can be held open. The screens are a Razor class library on `net11.0`, and the window's abilities interfaces (`IAppWindow`, `IFileDialogs`, `IPagePrinter`), so a spike of another host for them would say what's left.
  - **A scripted soak run** with the `run-tinapayanrms` driver, before each install at the shop: N rounds of the 20 addresses with `dotnet-gcdump` before and after, failing on growth in the live ViewModels or grids (the 2026-11-08 measurement, made repeatable). The smoke run is `driver.ps1 smoke` (2026-11-14); it doesn't yet answer the close question No and Cancel or read the log for new errors.
- **From the 2026-11-18 architecture review, waiting on the user's word, in the order it ranked them** (Batch 1 is done, in the log):
  - **Labor, overhead and administrative in pesos per batch, opt-in per product**, only if the measured percentages (the Todo at the top of this list) still misprice labor-heavy products. As percentages of the ingredients they follow ingredient prices: First Class Flour up 10% adds 0.108 of flour to a Pandesal Ordinary and 0.177 to its base cost.
  - **A way back from a bad update, and onto a new PC**: an older copy refuses a migrated database and the MSI refuses a downgrade, so rollback needs a script, and a new PC needs PostgreSQL's role and `pgpass.conf` set up (TinapayanBMS's `Setup-Database.ps1` is a start).
  - **Backups proved**: a weekly restore into a scratch database, and a banner while **Also Copy To** is empty.
  - **The Recipe API in the MSI**: it's published from source today (`Install-RecipeApi.ps1`), so it can fall behind the database.
  - **A data upgrade with no new migration never runs**: `DatabaseUpdater` and `DatabaseBackups.BringUpToDateAsync` skip `MigrateAsync`, which runs the upgrades, when no migration is pending.
  - **The page scripts' logic in the suite** (`app.js`, `vim.js`), which no automated test runs.
  - **Before two PCs save at once**: a unit of work takes its snapshot before the writes' lock, so beside a Read Committed save it can store a made ingredient's cost from a stale price. Not reproduced: a failing test comes first.
  - **`MarginPercent` named for the markup it holds**, keeping the stored JSON's name.
  - **For the owner**: a read-only Price Review ranked by pesos at stake (TinapayanBMS has the units made and left over), a market-day price sheet with a date each price was checked, and a daily cost history for every product.
